Title: The Accounts of the Wardens of the Parish of Morebath, Devon, 1520-1573
Creator: The parish of Morebath, Devon, as kept by its vicar Sir Christopher Trychay (J. Erskine Binney, Transcriber; F. W. Weaver, biographical index)
Publisher: Exeter: James G. Commin, 230 High Street
Publication Date: 1904
Rights: Public Domain (published 1904, public domain by date under the 95-year rule; archive.org possible-copyright-status NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT; Google Books digitisation with its public-domain notice retained at the head of the file)
Source: Internet Archive item accountswardens00weavgoog, file accountswardens00weavgoog_djvu.txt, fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: possible-copyright-status NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT; Google Books scan, its own public-domain usage notice retained at the head of the file; contributor University of Michigan
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged (zero long-s substitutions, the download contains no long-s glyph; nothing retyped, dropped or corrected; runs of spaces and page-number lines left as scanned). Rights basis restated: the title page reads Exeter, James G. Commin, 1904, so the volume is public domain by date.
Content note: The churchwardens' and store-wardens' accounts of one Devon parish, 1520-1573, printed in the original sixteenth-century spelling with the manuscript's abbreviations, preceded by Binney's introduction and followed by Weaver's biographical index. SECOND WITNESS for exact wording (Mark's OCR ruling, 2026-09-25): the Google OCR of this early-modern orthography misreads the superscript abbreviations systematically (for example "y8" or "y*" for the thorn-e "ye", "p'" for "per", roman numerals with stray characters), so a quote must be checked against the page images before it is relied on; the running English is nevertheless readable and searchable. OCR sampled at 10/30/50/70/90 per cent and found of that character throughout. No other public-domain edition of these accounts exists; Duffy's modern edition is in copyright.

INTRODUCTION. 

The  accounts  in  this  volume  are  printed  from  a  document 
first  brought  to  light  by  Rev.  Sackville  Berkeley,  when  Vicar 
of  the  parish  of  Morebath,  near  Bampton,  Devon,  to  which 
they  belong,  and  of  which  they  give  a  record  at  a  most 
interesting  period.  Mr.  Berkeley  contributed  a  short  paper 
on  this  document  to  the  proceedings  of  the  Somersetshire 
Archaeological  Society  in  the  year  1883. 

A  successor,  Rev.  J.  Erskine  Binney,  transcribed  the 
whole  of  the  MSS.  up  to  the  year  1573,  and,  by  the  kind- 
ness of  the  present  Vicar  of  Morebath,  Rev.  Annesley  Voysey, 
has  revised  his  transcript  by  the  original,  and  has  seen  the 
sheets  through  the  press. 

The  value  of  the  document  is : 

(a)  That  the  entries  comprise  a  period  of  great  importance 
in  the  Church  of  England  from  1520  to  1573. 

(b)  That  for  ail  this  time  it  was  written  by  one  person 
(except  in  one  year  when  a  few  lines  are  by  another 
hand),  the  Vicar,  Sir  Christopher  Trychay. 

(c)  The  number  of  stores  connected  with  the  Church,  and 
the  amount  of  property  belonging  to  each  store. 
There  were  eight  of  these,  viz.:  1,  S.  George,  the 
Patron  Saint ;  2,  Jesus ;  3,  Our  Lady  ;  4,  S.  Sidwell ; 
5,  S.  Antony ;  6,  The  Almslight ;  7,  the  young  men ; 
8,  the  young  women;  besides  a  company  of  three, 
four,  or  five  men,  who  seem  to  have  formed  a  kind 
of  Parish  Council,  and  received  the  surplus  from  the 
eight  stores  for  the  general  benefit  of  the  parish. 

(d)  The  great  interest  taken  by  the  parishioners  in  their 
Church  and  the  worship  therein. 

A  notable  instance  of  this  occurs  when  a  thief  broke  into 
the  Church  and  stole  a  chalice.  The  young  men  and  maidens 
immediately  undertook  to  collect  a  sufficient  sum  to  provide 
a  new  chalice.  The  list  of  vestments  is  large  for  a  small 
country  parish.  There  is  a  sort  of  pathos  in  the  story  of 
how  the  Vicar  had  devoted  his  wool  tithes  in  order  to  buy 


•  a  suit  of  black  vestments,  and  be  succeeded,  with  the  help  of 

some  timely  gifts  and  bequests,  in  procuring  them  just  a  year 
before  they  were  all  put  away  amongst  the  farmers  of  the 
parish,  to  avoid  confiscation  by  the  King's  Commissioners. 

The  reader  will  observe  two  interesting  notes  about  the 
Parish  Clerk  and  the  list  of  Dame  Rumbelow's  goods  and 
chattels,  which  gives  us  a  price  current  of  the  period.  The 
parish  was,  much  as  it  is  now,  cut  up  into  various  holdings, 

,  of  which  most  of  the  names  still  survive.    At  first  it  belonged 

to  the  Priory  of  Bar  lynch.  On  the  dissolution  of  these 
smaller  houses  in  1538  it  fell  into  the  hands  of  the  Powletts. 

■  Mr.  Hugh  Powlett's  name  occurs  more  than  once  in  the  MS. 

A  Glossarial  Index  containing  most  of  the  unusual  words 
is  appended  by  the  help  of  the  Rev.  F.  W.  Weaver,  m.a., 

I  Vicar  of  Milton,  Somerset ;  but  even  in  the  ordinary  words 

the   reader  will  have  to  take  account   of  Sir  Christopher 

J  Trychay's  peculiarities  of  spelling,  and  of  his  introduction 

of  little  bits  of  very  bad  Latin.     Moreover,  he  is  not  constant 

.  to  any  one  form  of  spelling  except  in  one  respect.    When 

*  an '  comes  before  a  word  commencing  with  a  vowel,  he 
joins  the  '  n  *  on  to  the  following  word,  e.g.,  we  find  a  nox, 
a  noke,  for  an  ox,  an  oak,  etc.

Sometime    in  the  year   1531   Sir    Christopher    Trychay 

bethought  him  to  draw  up  a  Roll,  containing  a  list  of  gifts 

and  benefactions  made  to  the  church  during  his  vicariate. 

He  commences  with  the  year  of  his  induction  to  the  benefice, 

as  follows : — 

Orate  p  animabus  sequentibus. 

Noubcne   M^  yl  here  after  schall  ye  see  &  knoo  how  y5  church  was 

p'vaylyd  by  y*  dethe  of  all  these  pssons  y1  here  after  ys 
exp'ssyd  by  name:  ye  wyche  all  &  synguler  geftis  was
